NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, May 10, 2016 
 
Present:  Steve Williams, Supervisor; Jesse Thompson, Vice Chairman; Jason Whiting, Chairman; Dawnafe 
Whitesinger, Supervisor; and Robert Black, Supervisor 
9:03 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation was led by Supervisor Whitesinger. 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no comments from the public. 
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes:  April 26, 2016 b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 April 2016 c) 
Constable Reports:  Show Low Precinct #5, April 2016 d) Approval of 1 Back Tax Land Parcel sold over the 
counter in the amount of $656.14 e) Special Event Liquor License for the Pinetop -Lakeside Chamber of 
Commerce charitable event to be held on June 18, 2016 at the Pinetop Lakes Activity Center & Stables in Pinetop 
f) Contract Signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution:  Frontier Services 
Agreement; and Professional Services Agreement with Interim Public Management for Consulting Fees g) 
Adoption of the Proposed FY2016 -17 Cost Allocation Schedule h) Intergovernmental Agreement between 
Navajo County Recorder and the Town of Snowflake to conduct city election for a span of 5 years to expire on 
January 1, 2021 i) Grant Agreement #HT -16-2614 with the City of Tucson for High Intensity Drug Trafficking 
Areas (HIDTA) funding for $129,151.00 j) App roval of the use of the southwest corner of the Navajo County 
Complex for the annual community July 4th Fireworks Display k) Memorandum of Understanding and Resource 
Sharing Agreement between Northeastern Arizona Innovative Workforce Solutions (NEAZIWS) and Workforce 
Innovation Opportunity Act Partners l) Name an unnamed existing road in the Shumway area to Morningside 
Road in Section 30 Township 12 North - Range 22 East m) Appoint Donna Hurstrom, Deputy Clerk of the Board, 
as an alternate Clerk of the Board to perform Clerk of the Board functions while the Clerk is away from the office 
n) Sympathy letter to Debby Kester and Lois Hancock for the loss of their mother: Supervisor Thompson made 
a motion to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the con sent agenda; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger.  Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES : Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions : Eric Scott 
reviewed the 30-item Personnel Action Report. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the Personnel 
Action Report as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
James Jayne stated that the 12 increases are for Public Works team members who are part of Red Card training. 
DRUG COURT:  Consideration and possible approval of a Proclamation declaring the month of May 2016 Drug 
Court Month: Tonya Reed, Navajo County Drug Court Coordinator, stated that Drug Court has been in existence 
for 11 years with approximately 80 graduates, 7  of which have been this year , and there are 27 active clients.  
Michala Ruechel, Presiding Judge of Superior Court, thanked the Board of Supervisors for their ongoing support 
of Drug Court and stated that most of the people who work in Drug Court do it vo luntarily in addition to their 
regular duties.  Judge Ruechel presented plaques to the following individuals:  Robert Higgins, Judge of the 
Superior Court; Dale Nielson, Judge of the Superior Court; Jason Moore, Deputy County Attorney; Anna Atencio, 
Deputy Public Defender; Michael Penrod, Public Defender; Chad Stears, Probation Officer; Tammie Begay, 
Surveillance Officer; Daniel Kakou, Surveillance Officer; Kristy Padilla, Community Counseling Center; Tonya 
Reed, Drug Court Coordinator; Sarah Medina, Community Bridges; and Brandt Clark, Deputy County Attorney.    
Brad Carlyon read the proposed proclamation declaring the month of May 2016 Drug Court Month.  Supervisor 
Whitesinger made a motion to approve a proclamation declaring the month of May 2016 Drug Cou rt Month;

<!-- page 2 -->
motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Mr. Carlyon recognized Sheriff 
Clark, Dale Nielson, and others as founding members of the Drug Court program in Navajo County.   He indicated 
that 70% of those in the criminal justice system suffer from substance abuse and/or mental health problems, and 
those issues need to be addressed; otherwise, the criminal justice system will continue to be a revolving door.  He 
spoke about how Drug Courts and other specialty courts ar e changing the dynamic, which improves the quality 
of life in our neighborhoods.  Dale Nielson, Superior Court Judge,  stated that it’s been a neat experience to be 
involved in Drug Court and expressed appreciation of those involved and the cooperation with Navajo County 
Attorney’s Office.  He spoke about the requirements of Drug Court for participants.  He thanked members of the 
Board, Sheriff Clark, Judge Higgins and Judge Ruechel for their support.  Supervisor Williams commended 
Judge Nielson for getting Drug Court started and agreed that there needs to be more aspects of rehabilitation in 
the criminal justice system and thanked all those who participate  in Drug Court .  Supervisor Whitesinger  
thanked the Drug Court Team for demonstrating pu blic service in the community.  Supervisor Thompson  
thanked the Drug Court Team and their efforts.  Supervisor Black expressed appreciation for the Drug Court 
Team for their dedication and commitment to help ing those with substance abuse issues.  Chairman Whiting 
thanked the Drug Court Team in their efforts to connect with the community and create change in Navajo County.   
He spoke about attending a Drug Court meeting a couple of months ago and saw that it was sincere and 
meaningful.  Mr. Carlyon stated th at there has been great success with Drug Court and that the judicial system 
has been trying to expand in other areas, including Veteran’s Court  and Mental Health Court.  Judge Ruechel 
stated that she is a big proponent of Drug Court and that Drug Court affects generations to come.  She thanked 
everyone for their hard work in this area. 
SHERIFF: Consideration and possible approval of a Proclamation declaring May 15-21, 2016, as National Police 
Week: Mr. Wolfe stated that he attended the Arizona Peace Officer’s Memorial and that Ronald Adair’s name 
was added who passed away on duty May 22, 1970 .  A video of Officer Adair’s recognition was presented.  
Chairman Whiting  read the proposed proclamation.   Supervisor Williams made a motion to approve a 
proclamation declaring May 15 -21, 2016, as National Police Week; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Mr. Carlyon stated that the Peace Officer’s Memorial was 
a solemn occasion.  He expressed how officers are our neighbors, that Sheriff’s deputies are encouraged to become 
involved in their neighborhoods , and that c ommunities need to embrace officers as our friends and neighbors 
when they wear their uniform.  He spoke about the dangers that officers f ace on a daily basis .  He spoke about 
Officer Varner who died in the line of duty and Deputy Adair who died in 1970 and was just added to the 
memorial.  He thanked Sheriff Clark and his officers for what they do.  Chairman Whiting  expressed 
appreciation for what officers do and the services that they render and that it’s disheartening that officers do not 
receive the appreciation they deserve from their communities .  Members of the Board of Supervisors and Mr. 
Carlyon presented the proclamation to Sheriff C lark.   Sheriff Clark thanked the Board for recognizing Police 
Officers week and for the kind words  of Mr. Carlyon.  He stated that he will be going to Washington with the 
Honor Guard to witness Deputy Adair being added to the Law Enforcement Memorial in W ashington.   
Supervisor Thompson thanked the Sheriff and his staff and expressed appreciation for their efforts in protecting 
and serving the public and that many of the elders tell people that policeme n are your friend and are there to 
protect and serve you. 
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
CONSENT AGENDA:  Supervisor Thompson made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Public Health 
Services District Board of Directors Session; motion seconded by  Supervisor Whitesinger.  Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. 
a. Amendment #3 of Contract #ADHS15 -094982 with the Arizona Depart ment of Health Services for the 
Healthy People Healthy Communities grant for July 1, 2016 to June 30, 2017 in the amount of $474,529.00

<!-- page 3 -->
b. Contract GRA-RC001-15-0694-01-Y2 with Arizona First Things First for the Navajo Nation Nutrition 
Education and Obesity Prevention Program for July 1, 2016 to June 30, 2017 in the amount of $582,930.00  
c. Contract GRA-RC015-16-0751-01-Y2 with Arizona First Things Fir st for the Navajo Apache Oral Health 
Program for July 1, 2016 through June 30, 2017 in the amount of $136,000.00 
d. Oral Health Fluoride Varnish Contract GRA -RC028-17-0825-01 for the White Mountain Apache Tribe 
Region with Arizona First Things First for July 1, 2016 through June 30, 2017 in the amount of $110,000.00  
e. Contract GRA-RC001-16-0750-01 with Arizona First Things First for Oral Health Services for Navajo Nation 
for July 1, 2016 to June 30, 2017 in the amount of $405,828.00 : Supervisor Thompson made a motion to 
authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
Supervisor Thompson made a motion to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Black.  Vote 
unanimous approving the motion. 
SUPERINTENDENT OF SCHOOLS: Consideration and possible approval of I ntergovernmental Agreement 
and Budget with the Governor’s Office of Youth, Faith and Family for a pilot substance abuse prevention program 
for Navajo County school districts:  Jalyn Gerlich, Superintendent of Schools,  discussed the item.  Supervisor 
Williams made a motion to approve the Intergovernmental Agreement and Budget with the Governor’s Office 
of Youth, Faith and Family for a pilot substance abus e prevention program for Navajo County school districts; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger.  Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
PUBLIC WORKS: 
a. Consideration and possible award of Contract #B16 -03-023 Supplying and Delivering Liquid Asphalt 
Products to Western Emulsions, Inc.; Hawker and Evans Asphalt Company, Inc.; and Cactus Transport, Inc.: 
Bill Bess discussed the item.  Supervisor Williams inquired as to why Cactus Transport was not awarded the 
contract as to Table One.  Mr. Bess stated that Cactus Transport is lowest overall in price for Table One, and 
he would have to work with staff to see if there was an underlying reason to award Western Emulsions the 
contract for Table One.  He stated that the decision could be put off until the next Board meeting and would 
not impact the schedule.   Supervisor Williams made a motion to table this item until the next Board 
meeting; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger.  Vote unanimous to table the item  until the next 
Board meeting. 
b. Consideration and possible award of Contract #B16 -04-001 Supply and Delivery of Culverts and Bands to 
Pacific Ponderosa Co :  Bill Bess discussed the item.   Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to award 
Contract B16-04-001 Supply and Delivery of Culverts and Bands to Pacific Ponderosa Co.; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Thompson.  Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
TREASURER: 
a. Consideration and possible acceptance of the resignation of Manny Hernandez, Navajo County Treasurer, 
effective May 27, 2016 : Manny Hernandez, Treasurer,  read his letter of retirement and resignation.  He 
expressed appreciation for his staff.  Supervisor Williams made a motion to accept the resignation of Manny 
Hernandez, Navajo County Treasurer, effective May 27, 2016; motion seconded by Supervisor Black.  Vote 
unanimous approving the motion .  Supervisor Thompson thanked Mr. Hernandez for his service to the 
community.  Mr. Hernandez thanked the Board of Supervisors for accepting his retirement and appointing 
Ms. Kester.   
b. Consideration and possible appointment of Debra Kester to fill the vacancy of M anny Hernandez, effective 
May 28, 2016: Mr. Hernandez requested that Debra Kester fill out the remainder of his term as Navajo County 
Treasurer.  Supervisor Thompson made a motion to appoint Debra Kester to fill the vacancy of Manny

<!-- page 4 -->
Hernandez, effective May 28, 2016; motion seconded by Supervisor Black.  Vote unanimous approving the 
motion. 
c. Presentation to Manny Hernandez for his 15.5+ years of serv ice to Navajo County : Supervisor Black 
expressed appreciation to Mr. Hernandez for being the type of manager he has always appreciated and for 
explaining his role as Treasurer to members of District One.  Debra Kester presented a Power Point 
presentation.  Supervisor Whitesinger thanked Mr. Hernandez for his example on what service is and how 
he has kept service for people at the forefront of what he does and also for protecting the dollars of community 
members.  Supervisor Williams recognized the confidence that Mr. Hernandez has shown in Ms. Kester.  He 
stated how Mr. Hernandez went above and beyond to help a disabled veteran be able to stay in his home and 
how Mr. Hernandez looked at each case individually.  He also expressed appreciation for Mr. Hernandez’s 
example on what an elected official should do.  Supervisor Thompson congratulated Ms. Kester on her 
appointment.  He stated that every other Tuesday, a bunch of retirees get together in Winslow, and now Mr. 
Hernandez is eligible to join.  Chairman Whiting congratulated Ms. Kester on her appointment.  He thanked 
Mr. Hernandez for being the kind man that he is , the leader that he is , and the good example that he is .  He 
stated he has received numerous phone calls from people who appreciate Mr. Hernandez’s service and are 
sorry to see him leave.  James Jayne, County Manager,  wished Mr. Hernandez and his wife all the best in 
retirement and that he looks forward to working with Ms. Kester.  The Board of Supervisors presented Mr. 
Hernandez with a retirement gift.  Mr. Hernandez thanked the Board of Supervisors, Mr. Jayne, the audience 
in the Chambers, and his staff.  Chairman Whiting noted that there will be a retirement party following the 
Board meeting. 
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE:  Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Whitesinger gave a report from District V.  She stated that she was a chaperone for her son’s field 
trip to Washington DC and Philadelphia which renewed for her what we do here and her love for her country.  
She gave accolades to Mr. Carlyon and his staff for the Victim Rights Symposium which she attended.  She stated 
that she was a key note speaker for Apache Families First and was also a key note speaker in Snowflake for Show 
Low Seniors, their Native American club.  She spoke about the inauguration of the White Mountain Apache Tribe 
this past Thursday and that there are four new council members, and she believes it will be a good council for the 
Tribe.  She indicated that she has been working with the Navajo County Drug Project to host a town hall at White 
Mountain Apache Tribe and applied for a grant to host that town hall where they will be discussing alcohol and 
substance abuse.  Supervisor Black gave a report from District I.  He thanked the supervisors and staff who went 
to Kayenta for the hospital dedication.  He also thanked Mr. Jayne, Mr. Carlyon, and Chairman Whiting for going 
to the senior center.  He thanked all those who attended the Economic Outlook Conference in Page.  He spoke 
about the 160 and 163 corridor and that he is working towards coming up with a plan to provide assistance to the 
organizations along that route .  He spoke about attending the candlelight vigil in Holbrook last Friday  for 
Ashlynne Mike and expressed appreciation for those who attended and for Mr. Carlyon’s words.  He stated that 
he will be concentrating on Red Dog graveling and getting the project in motion again.  Supervisor Williams 
gave a report from District IV.  He expressed appreciation to Adam Wolfe, Catrina Jenkins and Mary Springer 
for their help in presenting on forest issues and wildfire preparedness at the Show Low City Council.  He 
expressed appreciation for the good partnership with Show Low City Council.  He stated that he filmed Navajo 
County Connection.  He th anked those that have been in conversation with Pinetop Heritage Group and Heber-
Overgaard Senior Center.  He thanked the County Manager, Mr. Jayne, who he believes is the envy of all county 
managers across the State, for all he does and how accessible he is.  As to budget issues, he stated that from his 
perspective he gives great deference and respect to department heads to know what they need in their departments 
to succeed.  He further stated that money is finite, but it’s not the Board’s role to microma nage.  He expressed 
appreciation for the efforts of the department heads.  He recognized mothers throughout the county.  He stated 
that he attended a fathers and sons campout this past weekend. Supervisor Thompson gave a report from District

<!-- page 5 -->
II.  He concurred with Supervisor Williams with regard to the budget.  He stated that he recognized that the 
County does not have the money to do everything we would like to do and that we need to reach out to other 
sources, as well, and expressed appreciation to the department heads and elected officials during the budgeting 
season.  He spoke about lobbying for additional funding for school bus routes.  He spoke about the new health 
facility in Kayenta.  He expressed appreciation to Adam Wolfe for all the work that he does.  He spoke about the 
Economic Outlook Conference in Page and recognized that there will be challenges in the future, but all 
governmental entities have realized that they need to address the issues of people losing their jobs.  He spoke 
about attending the candlelight vigil for Ashlynne Mike in Holbrook and expressed appreciation for those who 
assisted in putting it together.  He thanked Brad Carlyon for his words at the vigil.  He indicated that he spoke at 
a Drug Court graduation.  Mr. Jayne th anked the Chairman , Supervisor Thompson, County Attorney, Sheriff 
Clark, and all those who have engaged with the legislature this session to lobby to have dollars returned to the 
County. Mr. Carl yon gave kudos to Supervisor Williams ’s son  for his achieveme nts at the White Mountain 
Regional Track Championships.  He stated that last Monday, Sheriff Clark, himself, and almost all of the other 
sheriffs and County attorneys in the State met with the DPS Director, because DPS wanted to take money from 
the Counties, and Mr. Carlyon explained to the DPS Director that the County is still feeling the effects of the last 
eight years of the economy and that DPS taking more money from Navajo County is wrong.  He indicated that 
two people have been indicted for embezzlement of monies from the Show Low Fire District and that there will 
be an official report from the Auditor General’s Office, which did the forensic accounting.  Chairman Whiting 
gave a report from District III.  He congratulated the Snowflake Boys Track Team for winning State for the second 
year in a row  and that he will meet with them today at 4:15 at the track field.  He thanked staff and fellow 
supervisors for the team effort with regards to the budget and constantly staying in front of the legislators to help 
them see that it is right to shift back responsibilities which the County has been bearing back to the State or shift 
funds from the State to the local level.  He stated concern regarding the election coming up and the challenge with 
new people coming.   He congratulated Melissa Buckley’s daughter, Elizabeth, who graduated from ASU today.  
He congratulated Manny Hernandez and his family.  He expressed appreciation for Supervisor Thompson, 
Supervisor Black, and Mr. Carlyon for attending the candlelight vigil in Holbrook.  He spoke about attending the 
Mr. Holbrook contest and indicated that the participants have been raising funds for the Ronald McDonald House 
and a local scholarship fund since February of this year and that they will continue to raise funds until the end of 
the school year.   Supervisor Thompson made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams.  
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
ADJOURN 10:46 a.m. 
APPROVED:       DATED: May 10, 2016 
 
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
ATTEST: 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors
